# Islam Dunk: every line of game text

Generated by `npm run review:export` from the game source. Mark up `game-text.csv` in a spreadsheet (Keep / Change / Cut, suggested text, notes), or use the in-game review mode (`?review=1`). Row numbers match the CSV.

4037 lines, 2902 new since the Phase 1 packet (marked **NEW**).

| 217 | `data/trivia.js:8 (trivia #4) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #4** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Quran itself says it was sent down in the month of Ramadan (Al-Baqarah 2:185). |
| 218 | `data/trivia.js:9 (trivia #5) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #5** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The first revelation came in the Cave of Hira, on Jabal an-Nur near Makkah. |
| 219 | `data/trivia.js:10 (trivia #6) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #6** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** What was the first word of the Quran revealed? Iqra' (Read). The first words revealed were the opening of Surah Al-Alaq: “Read in the name of your Lord.” |
| 220 | `data/trivia.js:11 (trivia #7) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #7** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** At-Tawbah is the only surah that does not begin with Bismillah. |
| 221 | `data/trivia.js:12 (trivia #8) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #8** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** An-Naml opens with Bismillah and has it again inside the letter of Sulayman (27:30). |
| 222 | `data/trivia.js:13 (trivia #9) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #9** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Ayat al-Kursi is verse 255 of Surah Al-Baqarah. |
| 223 | `data/trivia.js:14 (trivia #10) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #10** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Prophet ﷺ said Surah Al-Ikhlas equals a third of the Quran (Sahih al-Bukhari). |
| 224 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #11) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #11** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Quran is divided into 30 ajza’, so many people read one juz’ a day in Ramadan. |
| 225 | `data/trivia.js:16 (trivia #12) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #12** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Hijrah in 622 CE marks the start of the Hijri calendar. |
| 226 | `data/trivia.js:17 (trivia #13) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #13** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Prophet ﷺ was born in Makkah. |
| 227 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #14) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #14** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** He was born in the Year of the Elephant, when Abraha’s army marched on Makkah. |
| 228 | `data/trivia.js:19 (trivia #15) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #15** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Badr, in 2 AH, was the first major battle between the Muslims and Quraysh. |
| 229 | `data/trivia.js:20 (trivia #16) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #16** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Battle of Badr took place in Ramadan, 2 AH. |
| 230 | `data/trivia.js:21 (trivia #17) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #17** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** At Al-Khandaq in 5 AH, the Muslims dug a trench to defend Madinah. |
| 231 | `data/trivia.js:22 (trivia #18) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #18** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Treaty of Hudaybiyyah was agreed in 6 AH and brought a truce with Quraysh. |
| 232 | `data/trivia.js:23 (trivia #19) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #19** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Maryam is the only woman named in the Quran, and a surah carries her name. |
| 233 | `data/trivia.js:24 (trivia #20) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #20** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Five daily prayers are obligatory: Fajr, Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib and Isha. |
| 234 | `data/trivia.js:25 (trivia #21) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #21** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Which surah opens with the Night Journey? Al-Isra'. Surah Al-Isra opens with the Night Journey from Masjid al-Haram to Masjid al-Aqsa. |
| 235 | `data/trivia.js:26 (trivia #22) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #22** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Hadith encourage reciting Surah Al-Kahf on Friday. |
| 236 | `data/trivia.js:27 (trivia #23) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #23** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Al-Kahf tells of young believers who sheltered in a cave and slept for many years. |
| 237 | `data/trivia.js:28 (trivia #24) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #24** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Surah Al-Qadr says Laylatul Qadr is better than a thousand months (97:3). |
| 238 | `data/trivia.js:29 (trivia #25) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #25** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Masjid Quba, built on arrival near Madinah during the Hijrah, was the first masjid the Prophet ﷺ built. |
| 239 | `data/trivia.js:30 (trivia #26) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #26** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The qiblah was changed from Jerusalem to the Ka'bah in Makkah. The qiblah was changed from Jerusalem to the Ka’bah in 2 AH (Al-Baqarah 2:144). |
| 240 | `data/trivia.js:31 (trivia #27) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #27** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The revelation came over about 23 years. |
| 241 | `data/trivia.js:32 (trivia #28) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #28** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The angel Jibril brought the revelation to the Prophet ﷺ. |
| 242 | `data/trivia.js:33 (trivia #29) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #29** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Al-Fatihah has seven ayat and is recited in every rak’ah of salah. |
| 243 | `data/trivia.js:34 (trivia #30) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #30** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** An-Nas is the 114th and final surah in the order of the Mushaf. |
| 244 | `data/trivia.js:35 (trivia #31) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #31** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Musa is named more than any other prophet in the Quran, over 130 times. |
| 245 | `data/trivia.js:36 (trivia #32) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #32** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Surah Yusuf (12:3) calls its account “the best of stories.” |
| 246 | `data/trivia.js:37 (trivia #33) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #33** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** An-Nahl means “The Bee,” and the surah mentions honey as a healing for people (16:69). |
| 247 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #34) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #34** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The first revelation came when the Prophet ﷺ was about 40 years old. |
| 248 | `data/trivia.js:39 (trivia #35) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #35** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Madinah was called Yathrib before the Hijrah. |
| 249 | `data/trivia.js:40 (trivia #36) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #36** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Which surah is named after the day of congregational prayer? Al-Jumu'ah. Surah Al-Jumu’ah calls believers to hurry to the Friday prayer (62:9). |
| 250 | `data/trivia.js:41 (trivia #37) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #37** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Surah Al-Fil tells the story of the army of the Elephant. Surah Al-Fil recalls how Allah protected the Ka’bah from Abraha’s army with elephants. |
| 251 | `data/trivia.js:42 (trivia #38) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #38** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Ar-Rahman repeats “Which of the favors of your Lord will you deny?” 31 times. |
| 252 | `data/trivia.js:43 (trivia #39) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #39** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Al-Baqarah is named after the story of the cow in ayat 67 to 73. |
| 253 | `data/trivia.js:45 (trivia #40) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #40** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The five pillars are the shahadah, salah, zakah, fasting Ramadan and Hajj (Sahih al-Bukhari and Muslim). |
| 254 | `data/trivia.js:46 (trivia #41) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #41** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** How many pillars of iman (faith) are there? 6. Belief in Allah, His angels, His books, His messengers, the Last Day and qadar, from the hadith of Jibril (Sahih Muslim). |
| 255 | `data/trivia.js:47 (trivia #42) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #42** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The rites of Hajj take place in the early days of Dhul-Hijjah, the last month of the Hijri year. |
| 256 | `data/trivia.js:48 (trivia #43) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #43** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** For how long must savings be held before zakah is due on them? One lunar year. Zakah is due on savings above the nisab once they have been held for a full lunar year (the hawl). |
| 257 | `data/trivia.js:49 (trivia #44) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #44** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Zakah on money and trade goods is one-fortieth, or 2.5%. |
| 258 | `data/trivia.js:50 (trivia #45) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #45** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The nisab is the threshold; below it, no zakah is due. |
| 259 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #46) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #46** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Fajr is two rak'ahs. |
| 260 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #47) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #47** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Maghrib is the only obligatory prayer with three rak'ahs. |
| 261 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #48) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #48** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Dhuhr, Asr and Isha are four rak'ahs each; a traveler shortens them to two. |
| 262 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #49) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #49** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Fajr 2, Dhuhr 4, Asr 4, Maghrib 3 and Isha 4 add up to 17. |

| 264 | `data/trivia.js:56 (trivia #51) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #51** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Duha is a voluntary morning prayer; the five obligatory ones are Fajr, Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib and Isha. |
| 265 | `data/trivia.js:57 (trivia #52) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #52** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The adhan announces the prayer time; the iqamah is the second call when the prayer is about to start. |
| 266 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #53) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #53** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Who was the first mu'adhdhin (caller to prayer) in Islam? Bilal ibn Rabah. The Prophet ﷺ chose Bilal, known for his strong, beautiful voice, to call the adhan in Madinah. |
| 267 | `data/trivia.js:59 (trivia #54) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #54** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Wudu, the ablution, washes the hands, mouth, nose, face, arms, head (wiped) and feet (Al-Ma'idah 5:6). |

| 270 | `data/trivia.js:62 (trivia #57) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #57** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Tarawih are the extra night prayers many masjids pray in congregation during Ramadan. |
| 271 | `data/trivia.js:63 (trivia #58) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #58** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Prophet ﷺ said, “Eat suhur, for in suhur there is blessing” (Sahih al-Bukhari). |

| 273 | `data/trivia.js:65 (trivia #60) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #60** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Eid al-Fitr is on the 1st of Shawwal, right after Ramadan. |
| 274 | `data/trivia.js:66 (trivia #61) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #61** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Eid al-Adha is the 10th of Dhul-Hijjah, the day after 'Arafah. |
| 275 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #62) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #62** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Pilgrims stand at 'Arafah on the 9th of Dhul-Hijjah, the heart of Hajj. |

| 277 | `data/trivia.js:69 (trivia #64) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #64** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Prophet ﷺ used to do i'tikaf in the last ten days of Ramadan (Sahih al-Bukhari). |
| 278 | `data/trivia.js:70 (trivia #65) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #65** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** In which nights is Laylatul Qadr especially sought? The last ten nights of Ramadan. The Prophet ﷺ said to seek it in the odd nights of the last ten of Ramadan (Sahih al-Bukhari). |
| 279 | `data/trivia.js:71 (trivia #66) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #66** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Hijri months follow the moon, so Ramadan lasts 29 or 30 days. |
| 280 | `data/trivia.js:72 (trivia #67) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #67** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Hijri year begins with Muharram. |
| 281 | `data/trivia.js:73 (trivia #68) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #68** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Quran says the number of months with Allah is twelve (At-Tawbah 9:36). |
| 282 | `data/trivia.js:74 (trivia #69) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #69** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Ramadan is which month of the Hijri calendar? The 9th. Ramadan is the ninth month, between Sha'ban and Shawwal. |
| 283 | `data/trivia.js:75 (trivia #70) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #70** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Shawwal follows Ramadan, and Eid al-Fitr is its first day. |

| 285 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #72) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #72** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** 'Ashura is the 10th of Muharram; the Prophet ﷺ fasted it and encouraged fasting it. |
| 286 | `data/trivia.js:78 (trivia #73) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #73** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Who was the first caliph after the Prophet ﷺ? Abu Bakr as-Siddiq. Abu Bakr led the Muslims after the Prophet ﷺ passed away in 11 AH. |
| 287 | `data/trivia.js:79 (trivia #74) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #74** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Which caliph had standard copies of the Quran sent to the major cities? Uthman ibn Affan. Under Uthman, standardized copies of the Mushaf were made and sent out to the Muslim cities. |
| 288 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #75) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #75** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Abu Bakr hid with him in the cave, “the second of the two” (At-Tawbah 9:40). |
| 289 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #76) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #76** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Who slept in the Prophet's ﷺ bed on the night of the Hijrah? Ali ibn Abi Talib. Young Ali stayed behind in his bed, then returned the trusts people had left with the Prophet ﷺ. |
| 290 | `data/trivia.js:82 (trivia #77) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #77** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Khadijah bint Khuwaylid was his first wife and the first to believe in his message. |
| 291 | `data/trivia.js:83 (trivia #78) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #78** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Which companion was given the title al-Faruq? Umar ibn al-Khattab. Umar was called al-Faruq, the one who distinguishes truth from falsehood. |
| 292 | `data/trivia.js:84 (trivia #79) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #79** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Prophet ﷺ gave Khalid ibn al-Walid the title Sword of Allah. |
| 293 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #80) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #80** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Hamzah ibn Abd al-Muttalib, the Lion of Allah, was martyred at Uhud. |
| 294 | `data/trivia.js:86 (trivia #81) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #81** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Abu Talib took him in at about age eight and protected him for years. |
| 295 | `data/trivia.js:87 (trivia #82) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #82** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Abd al-Muttalib cared for him after his mother died. |
| 296 | `data/trivia.js:88 (trivia #83) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #83** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** His mother was Aminah bint Wahb; she passed away when he was about six. |
| 297 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #84) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #84** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Who nursed the Prophet ﷺ as a baby in the desert? Halimah as-Sa'diyyah. Halimah of the Banu Sa'd nursed him in the desert. |
| 298 | `data/trivia.js:90 (trivia #85) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #85** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Who is known as the first martyr in Islam? Sumayyah bint Khayyat. Sumayyah, the mother of Ammar ibn Yasir, was killed in Makkah for refusing to give up her faith. |
| 299 | `data/trivia.js:91 (trivia #86) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #86** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** After the first Pledge of 'Aqabah, Mus'ab ibn Umayr went to teach the Quran in Yathrib. |
| 300 | `data/trivia.js:92 (trivia #87) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #87** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Salman al-Farisi suggested the trench, a Persian defense the Arabs had not used before. |
| 301 | `data/trivia.js:93 (trivia #88) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #88** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Abu Hurayrah narrated more hadith than any other companion. |
| 302 | `data/trivia.js:94 (trivia #89) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #89** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Fatimah married Ali ibn Abi Talib; their sons were al-Hasan and al-Husayn. |
| 303 | `data/trivia.js:96 (trivia #91) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #91** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Battle of Uhud took place in 3 AH beside Mount Uhud. |
| 304 | `data/trivia.js:97 (trivia #92) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #92** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Makkah was opened in Ramadan of 8 AH, with a general amnesty. |
| 305 | `data/trivia.js:98 (trivia #93) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #93** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Hunayn was fought in 8 AH, just after the opening of Makkah. |
| 306 | `data/trivia.js:99 (trivia #94) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #94** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The expedition to Tabuk was in 9 AH. |
| 307 | `data/trivia.js:100 (trivia #95) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #95** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Farewell Hajj was in 10 AH, a few months before he passed away. |
| 308 | `data/trivia.js:101 (trivia #96) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #96** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** About 13 years in Makkah, then about 10 in Madinah. |
| 309 | `data/trivia.js:101 (trivia #97) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #97** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** He lived in Madinah for about ten years, from 1 AH to 11 AH. |
| 310 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #98) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #98** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** He passed away in Madinah in 11 AH at the age of 63. |
| 311 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #99) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #99** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** He is buried in Madinah, in what was 'Aishah's room, now within Masjid an-Nabawi. |
| 312 | `data/trivia.js:105 (trivia #100) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #100** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** In about the fifth year of prophethood, a group migrated to Abyssinia (Habashah). |
| 313 | `data/trivia.js:106 (trivia #101) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #101** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** An-Najashi (the Negus) gave the Muslim refugees his protection. |
| 314 | `data/trivia.js:107 (trivia #102) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #102** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Which companion spoke for the Muslims before the king of Abyssinia? Ja'far ibn Abi Talib. Ja'far explained Islam and recited from Surah Maryam, and the king refused to hand them over. |
| 315 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #103) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #103** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** In Ta'if he was rejected and stoned, yet he made du'a for their descendants to believe. |
| 316 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #104) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #104** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Pledges of 'Aqabah were made with people from which city? Yathrib (Madinah). People of Yathrib pledged at 'Aqabah, which opened the way to the Hijrah. |
| 317 | `data/trivia.js:110 (trivia #105) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #105** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Al-Ansar means the helpers. |
| 318 | `data/trivia.js:111 (trivia #106) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #106** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Al-Muhajirun means the emigrants. |
| 319 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #107) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #107** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** What did the Prophet ﷺ set up between the Muhajirun and the Ansar in Madinah? Pairs of brothers. He paired emigrants and helpers as brothers, and many Ansar shared their homes and wealth. |
| 320 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #108) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #108** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The five daily prayers were made obligatory on the night of the Mi'raj (Sahih al-Bukhari). |
| 321 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #109) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #109** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Al-Isra' is the night journey to Jerusalem; al-Mi'raj is the ascension that followed. |
| 322 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #110) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #110** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Masjid an-Nabawi, the Prophet's Masjid, was built soon after the Hijrah. |
| 323 | `data/trivia.js:116 (trivia #111) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #111** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Which masjid in Madinah is named after the change of qiblah? Masjid al-Qiblatayn. Al-Qiblatayn means the two qiblahs. |
| 324 | `data/trivia.js:117 (trivia #112) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #112** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** After Masjid al-Haram and Masjid an-Nabawi, Masjid al-Aqsa in Jerusalem is the third. |
| 325 | `data/trivia.js:118 (trivia #113) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #113** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The khatib stands on the minbar. A carved minbar is one of our masjid projects. |
| 326 | `data/trivia.js:119 (trivia #114) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #114** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The mihrab marks the qiblah wall; the imam leads the prayer in front of it. |
| 327 | `data/trivia.js:120 (trivia #115) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #115** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The minaret lets the adhan carry across the neighborhood. |
| 328 | `data/trivia.js:121 (trivia #116) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #116** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The well of Zamzam sprang up for Hajar and baby Isma'il. |
| 329 | `data/trivia.js:122 (trivia #117) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #117** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Ibrahim and Isma'il raised its foundations (Al-Baqarah 2:127). |
| 330 | `data/trivia.js:123 (trivia #118) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #118** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Pilgrims walk between Safa and Marwah seven times in the sa'y. |
| 331 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #119) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #119** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Tawaf is circling the Ka'bah. |
| 332 | `data/trivia.js:125 (trivia #120) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #120** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** One tawaf is seven circuits. |

| 335 | `data/trivia.js:128 (trivia #123) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #123** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Yunus called on Allah from the belly of the fish and was saved (As-Saffat 37:139-148). |

| 337 | `data/trivia.js:130 (trivia #125) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #125** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** “O fire, be coolness and safety upon Ibrahim” (Al-Anbiya' 21:69). |
| 338 | `data/trivia.js:131 (trivia #126) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #126** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Ayyub stayed patient and Allah restored him (Sad 38:41-44). |
| 339 | `data/trivia.js:132 (trivia #127) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #127** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Sulayman heard the ant warn the others and smiled (An-Naml 27:16-19). |
| 340 | `data/trivia.js:133 (trivia #128) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #128** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** “And to Dawud We gave the Zabur” (An-Nisa' 4:163). |
| 341 | `data/trivia.js:134 (trivia #129) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #129** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Tawrah was revealed to Musa. |

| 343 | `data/trivia.js:136 (trivia #131) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #131** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Hud was sent to 'Ad (Al-A'raf 7:65). |
| 344 | `data/trivia.js:137 (trivia #132) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #132** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Salih was sent to Thamud, and the she-camel was their sign (Al-A'raf 7:73). |
| 345 | `data/trivia.js:136 (trivia #133) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #133** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Shu'ayb told Madyan to give full measure and weight (Al-A'raf 7:85). |
| 346 | `data/trivia.js:139 (trivia #134) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #134** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Adam, the first human, was also the first prophet. |

| 349 | `data/trivia.js:142 (trivia #137) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #137** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** “And Allah spoke to Musa directly” (An-Nisa' 4:164). |
| 350 | `data/trivia.js:143 (trivia #138) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #138** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Musa asked for his brother Harun to support him (Ta-Ha 20:29-32). A good teammate. |
| 351 | `data/trivia.js:144 (trivia #139) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #139** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Yusuf explained the dream, and Egypt prepared for the famine (Yusuf 12:43-49). |
| 352 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #140) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #140** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Musa's staff became a snake before Pharaoh (Ta-Ha 20:19-21). |
| 353 | `data/trivia.js:146 (trivia #141) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #141** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** For which prophet did Allah make iron soft? Dawud. “And We made iron soft for him” (Saba' 34:10). |
| 354 | `data/trivia.js:147 (trivia #142) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #142** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** 'Isa was born to Maryam without a father (Aal-'Imran 3:47). |
| 355 | `data/trivia.js:148 (trivia #143) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #143** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Allah gave Zakariyya the good news of a son named Yahya in his old age (Maryam 19:7). |
| 356 | `data/trivia.js:149 (trivia #144) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #144** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Which prophet is called the seal of the prophets? Muhammad ﷺ. He is the Messenger of Allah and the seal of the prophets (Al-Ahzab 33:40). |
| 357 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #145) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #145** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Quran calls the Prophet ﷺ a mercy to all the worlds. “And We have not sent you except as a mercy to the worlds” (Al-Anbiya' 21:107). |
| 358 | `data/trivia.js:151 (trivia #146) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #146** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** “And indeed, you are of a great moral character” (Al-Qalam 68:4). |
| 359 | `data/trivia.js:152 (trivia #147) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #147** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Al-Baqarah is the second surah. |
| 360 | `data/trivia.js:153 (trivia #148) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #148** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Prophet ﷺ called Al-Baqarah and Aal-'Imran the two bright ones (Sahih Muslim). |
| 361 | `data/trivia.js:154 (trivia #149) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #149** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Al-Falaq and An-Nas together are called al-Mu'awwidhatayn. The two surahs of seeking refuge, often recited for protection. |
| 362 | `data/trivia.js:155 (trivia #150) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #150** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Surah Al-Mulk (67) begins “Tabaraka alladhi biyadihil-mulk.” |
| 363 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #151) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #151** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Surah Al-Ma'idah is named after a table spread with food. It is named after the table sent down, asked for by the disciples of 'Isa (5:112-115). |
| 364 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #152) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #152** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Surah Al-'Ankabut is named after which creature? The spider. It compares false protectors to a spider's house, the weakest of houses (29:41). |
| 365 | `data/trivia.js:158 (trivia #153) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #153** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** It tells of the ant who warned the others as Sulayman's army came (27:18). |
| 366 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #154) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #154** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Al-Hadid means iron, which the surah mentions as having great strength (57:25). |
| 367 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #155) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #155** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** It opens: “Nun. By the pen and what they write” (68:1). |
| 368 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #156) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #156** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** “By time, indeed mankind is in loss”, except those who believe, do good and advise each other to truth and patience (103). |
| 369 | `data/trivia.js:162 (trivia #157) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #157** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Surah Quraysh (106) reminds them of the trade journeys Allah made safe for them. |
| 370 | `data/trivia.js:163 (trivia #158) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #158** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Luqman advises his son on tawhid, salah, patience and humility (31:13-19). |
| 371 | `data/trivia.js:164 (trivia #159) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #159** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Dhul-Qarnayn's journeys and the wall he built are in Al-Kahf (18:83-98). |
| 372 | `data/trivia.js:165 (trivia #160) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #160** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** “Allah is the Light of the heavens and the earth” (An-Nur 24:35). |
| 373 | `data/trivia.js:166 (trivia #161) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #161** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** At-Tin (95) begins “By the fig and the olive.” |
| 374 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #162) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #162** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Surah Al-'Adiyat opens by swearing by charging horses. Al-'Adiyat means the charging (horses) that run panting (100:1). |
| 375 | `data/trivia.js:168 (trivia #163) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #163** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Al-Ma'un (107) connects faith with caring for the orphan and the poor. |
| 376 | `data/trivia.js:169 (trivia #164) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #164** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Surah 111 is known as both Al-Masad and Al-Lahab. |
| 377 | `data/trivia.js:170 (trivia #165) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #165** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Which is the longest ayah in the Quran? The debt ayah (2:282). Al-Baqarah 2:282, about writing down debts, is the longest ayah. |
| 378 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #167) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #167** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** What does 'Subhanallah' mean? Glory be to Allah. Subhanallah declares Allah free of every imperfection. |
| 379 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #168) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #168** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** What does 'Alhamdulillah' mean? All praise is for Allah. Alhamdulillah, the first words after the basmalah in Al-Fatihah. |
| 380 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #169) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #169** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** What does 'In sha Allah' mean? If Allah wills. The Quran teaches us to say in sha Allah about the future (Al-Kahf 18:23-24). |
| 381 | `data/trivia.js:175 (trivia #170) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #170** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Masha'Allah praises Allah for the good you see (Al-Kahf 18:39). |
| 382 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #171) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #171** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** What does 'Astaghfirullah' mean? I seek Allah's forgiveness. The Prophet ﷺ sought Allah's forgiveness more than seventy times a day (Sahih al-Bukhari). |
| 383 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #172) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #172** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** What is the reply to 'Assalamu alaikum'? Wa alaikum assalam. The Quran says to return a greeting with a better one or at least the same (An-Nisa' 4:86). |
| 384 | `data/trivia.js:178 (trivia #173) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #173** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** What do you say to thank someone, meaning 'may Allah reward you with good'? Jazakallahu khayran. The Prophet ﷺ taught this as an excellent way to thank someone (Sunan at-Tirmidhi). |
| 385 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #174) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #174** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** What does the word 'Islam' mean? Submission to Allah. Islam means submitting to Allah, and it shares a root with salam, peace. |
| 386 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #175) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #175** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** What does 'Quran' literally mean? The recitation. Quran comes from qara'a, to read or recite. |
| 387 | `data/trivia.js:0 (trivia #176) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #176** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** The Sunnah is what he said, did and approved. |
| 388 | `data/trivia.js:182 (trivia #177) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #177** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Tafsir explains the meanings of the Quran. |
| 389 | `data/trivia.js:183 (trivia #178) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #178** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Tajwid covers pronunciation, lengthening and pauses. |
| 390 | `data/trivia.js:185 (trivia #180) + career/office.js TRIVIA_WHY` | **trivia #180** **REVIEW 9.4** **NEW** Sadaqah can be anything good, even a smile (Sunan at-Tirmidhi). |

| 942 | `career/office.js:60` | The Quran has 114 surahs, from Al-Fatihah to An-Nas. |
| 943 | `career/office.js:61` | Al-Fatihah is called Umm al-Kitab because it opens the Quran and gathers its core themes. |

| 945 | `career/office.js:63` | Al-Kawthar has only three short ayat, making it the shortest surah. |

| 948 | `career/office.js:66` | The first words revealed were the opening of Surah Al-Alaq: “Read in the name of your Lord.” |

| 950 | `career/office.js:68` | An-Naml opens with Bismillah and has it again inside the letter of Sulayman (27:30). |
| 951 | `career/office.js:69` | Ayat al-Kursi is verse 255 of Surah Al-Baqarah. |
| 952 | `career/office.js:70` | The Prophet ﷺ said Surah Al-Ikhlas equals a third of the Quran (Sahih al-Bukhari). |
| 953 | `career/office.js:71` | The Quran is divided into 30 ajza’, so many people read one juz’ a day in Ramadan. |
| 954 | `career/office.js:72` | The Hijrah in 622 CE marks the start of the Hijri calendar. |
| 955 | `career/office.js:73` | The Prophet ﷺ was born in Makkah. |
| 956 | `career/office.js:74` | He was born in the Year of the Elephant, when Abraha’s army marched on Makkah. |
| 957 | `career/office.js:75` | Badr, in 2 AH, was the first major battle between the Muslims and Quraysh. |
| 958 | `career/office.js:76` | The Battle of Badr took place in Ramadan, 2 AH. |
| 959 | `career/office.js:77` | At Al-Khandaq in 5 AH, the Muslims dug a trench to defend Madinah. |
| 960 | `career/office.js:78` | The Treaty of Hudaybiyyah was agreed in 6 AH and brought a truce with Quraysh. |
| 961 | `career/office.js:79` | Maryam is the only woman named in the Quran, and a surah carries her name. |
| 962 | `career/office.js:80` | Five daily prayers are obligatory: Fajr, Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib and Isha. |
| 963 | `career/office.js:81` | Surah Al-Isra opens with the Night Journey from Masjid al-Haram to Masjid al-Aqsa. |
| 964 | `career/office.js:82` | Hadith encourage reciting Surah Al-Kahf on Friday. |
| 965 | `career/office.js:83` | Al-Kahf tells of young believers who sheltered in a cave and slept for many years. |
| 966 | `career/office.js:84` | Surah Al-Qadr says Laylatul Qadr is better than a thousand months (97:3). |
| 967 | `career/office.js:85` | Masjid Quba, built on arrival near Madinah during the Hijrah, was the first masjid the Prophet ﷺ built. |
| 968 | `career/office.js:86` | The qiblah was changed from Jerusalem to the Ka’bah in 2 AH (Al-Baqarah 2:144). |
| 969 | `career/office.js:87` | The revelation came over about 23 years. |
| 970 | `career/office.js:88` | The angel Jibril brought the revelation to the Prophet ﷺ. |
| 971 | `career/office.js:89` | Al-Fatihah has seven ayat and is recited in every rak’ah of salah. |
| 972 | `career/office.js:90` | An-Nas is the 114th and final surah in the order of the Mushaf. |
| 973 | `career/office.js:91` | Musa is named more than any other prophet in the Quran, over 130 times. |
| 974 | `career/office.js:92` | Surah Yusuf (12:3) calls its account “the best of stories.” |
| 975 | `career/office.js:93` | An-Nahl means “The Bee,” and the surah mentions honey as a healing for people (16:69). |
| 976 | `career/office.js:94` | The first revelation came when the Prophet ﷺ was about 40 years old. |
| 977 | `career/office.js:95` | Madinah was called Yathrib before the Hijrah. |
| 978 | `career/office.js:96` | Surah Al-Jumu’ah calls believers to hurry to the Friday prayer (62:9). |
| 979 | `career/office.js:97` | Surah Al-Fil recalls how Allah protected the Ka’bah from Abraha’s army with elephants. |
| 980 | `career/office.js:98` | Ar-Rahman repeats “Which of the favors of your Lord will you deny?” 31 times. |
| 981 | `career/office.js:99` | Al-Baqarah is named after the story of the cow in ayat 67 to 73. |
| 982 | `career/office.js:100` | The five pillars are the shahadah, salah, zakah, fasting Ramadan and Hajj (Sahih al-Bukhari and Muslim). |
| 983 | `career/office.js:101` | Belief in Allah, His angels, His books, His messengers, the Last Day and qadar, from the hadith of Jibril (Sahih Muslim). |
| 984 | `career/office.js:102` | The rites of Hajj take place in the early days of Dhul-Hijjah, the last month of the Hijri year. |
| 985 | `career/office.js:103` | Zakah is due on savings above the nisab once they have been held for a full lunar year (the hawl). |
| 986 | `career/office.js:104` | Zakah on money and trade goods is one-fortieth, or 2.5%. |
| 987 | `career/office.js:106` | Fajr is two rak'ahs. |
| 988 | `career/office.js:107` | Maghrib is the only obligatory prayer with three rak'ahs. |
| 989 | `career/office.js:109` | Fajr 2, Dhuhr 4, Asr 4, Maghrib 3 and Isha 4 add up to 17. |
| 990 | `career/office.js:110` | Maghrib begins when the sun has set. |
| 991 | `career/office.js:113` | The Prophet ﷺ chose Bilal, known for his strong, beautiful voice, to call the adhan in Madinah. |

| 993 | `career/office.js:115` | When water can't be used, the Quran allows tayammum with clean earth (An-Nisa' 4:43, Al-Ma'idah 5:6). |
| 994 | `career/office.js:116` | The Friday prayer, with its khutbah, is prayed at Dhuhr time in congregation. |
| 995 | `career/office.js:117` | Tarawih are the extra night prayers many masjids pray in congregation during Ramadan. |
| 996 | `career/office.js:118` | The Prophet ﷺ said, “Eat suhur, for in suhur there is blessing” (Sahih al-Bukhari). |
| 997 | `career/office.js:120` | Eid al-Fitr is on the 1st of Shawwal, right after Ramadan. |
| 998 | `career/office.js:121` | Eid al-Adha is the 10th of Dhul-Hijjah, the day after 'Arafah. |
| 999 | `career/office.js:122` | Pilgrims stand at 'Arafah on the 9th of Dhul-Hijjah, the heart of Hajj. |
| 1000 | `career/office.js:123` | Zakat al-Fitr is paid before the Eid prayer so everyone can celebrate Eid. |

| 1003 | `career/office.js:126` | Hijri months follow the moon, so Ramadan lasts 29 or 30 days. |
| 1004 | `career/office.js:127` | The Hijri year begins with Muharram. |
| 1005 | `career/office.js:128` | The Quran says the number of months with Allah is twelve (At-Tawbah 9:36). |
| 1006 | `career/office.js:129` | Ramadan is the ninth month, between Sha'ban and Shawwal. |
| 1007 | `career/office.js:130` | Shawwal follows Ramadan, and Eid al-Fitr is its first day. |

| 1023 | `career/office.js:148` | Abu Hurayrah narrated more hadith than any other companion. |
| 1024 | `career/office.js:150` | The people of Makkah called him al-Amin. Amanah, trust, comes from the same root as our masjid's name. |
| 1025 | `career/office.js:151` | The Battle of Uhud took place in 3 AH beside Mount Uhud. |
| 1026 | `career/office.js:152` | Makkah was opened in Ramadan of 8 AH, with a general amnesty. |

| 1028 | `career/office.js:154` | The expedition to Tabuk was in 9 AH. |
| 1029 | `career/office.js:155` | The Farewell Hajj was in 10 AH, a few months before he passed away. |

| 1162 | `career/office.js:284` | Keep winning like you’re still the underdog. |
| 1163 | `career/office.js:286` | Release at the top of your jump. A calm shooter is a better shooter. |
| 1164 | `career/office.js:287` | The extra pass is a gift. Give it freely and the open shot comes back to you. |
| 1165 | `career/office.js:288` | On defense, stay between your man and the rim. Make him take the hard shot. |
| 1166 | `career/office.js:289` | Box out first, then go get the ball. Position beats height. |
| 1167 | `career/office.js:290` | When a defender is in your face, move the ball. When you’re open, shoot with confidence. |
| 1168 | `career/office.js:291` | Free throws are won in practice, not in the game. |
| 1169 | `career/office.js:292` | Don’t reach for steals all night. Good defense is mostly your feet. |
| 1170 | `career/office.js:293` | Sprinting is a tool, not a habit. Save your legs for the moments that matter. |
| 1171 | `career/office.js:294` | Renew your intention before every game. The Prophet ﷺ taught that actions are judged by intentions. |
| 1172 | `career/office.js:295` | Sabr isn’t only for hard days. It’s also staying steady when things go well. |
| 1173 | `career/office.js:296` | Allah is with the patient. That’s in Surah Al-Baqarah (2:153). |
| 1174 | `career/office.js:297` | Humility after a win protects the heart. Thank Allah first, then your teammates. |
| 1175 | `career/office.js:298` | The believers are like one building, each part holding up the other. That’s a team. |
| 1176 | `career/office.js:299` | Smile at your brother. The Prophet ﷺ taught that a smile is charity. |
| 1177 | `career/office.js:300` | Whatever happens on the court, don’t let it make you late for salah. |

| 1179 | `career/office.js:302` | The strong one isn’t the one who overpowers others, but the one who controls himself when angry. |
| 1180 | `career/office.js:303` | Say bismillah before you start and alhamdulillah when you finish, win or lose. |
| 1181 | `career/office.js:304` | Talk on defense. A quiet team gets beat by screens. |
| 1182 | `career/office.js:305` | If you miss, sprint back. The next play is the only one you can change. |
| 1183 | `career/office.js:306` | Rest, eat well, and sleep. Your body is an amanah, a trust. |
| 1184 | `career/office.js:308` | A crossover works best when the defender is leaning. Read his hips, not his hands. |

| 1188 | `career/office.js:312` | When you’re tired, your shot gets short. Legs first, then arms. |
| 1189 | `career/office.js:313` | **NEW** A cheap foul might feel good for a second. It costs the team for the rest of the game. |
| 1190 | `career/office.js:314` | Take the charge. Standing still in the right spot is a skill. |

| 1192 | `career/office.js:316` | A dunk counts two, the same as a layup. Pick the one that goes in. |

| 1195 | `career/office.js:319` | Late in close games, slow down. Rushing is how good teams lose. |

| 1198 | `career/office.js:322` | Stamina is built in the off days. The last minute belongs to whoever trained for it. |
| 1199 | `career/office.js:323` | Know your teammate’s favorite spot and get him the ball there. |
| 1200 | `career/office.js:324` | A good screen is an act of service. You get hit so your brother can get free. |
| 1201 | `career/office.js:325` | When the defense collapses on you, the open man is behind them. Look for him. |
| 1202 | `career/office.js:326` | Hot hand? Keep shooting good shots, not just any shots. |
| 1203 | `career/office.js:327` | The shot clock is a friend. Use it, don’t panic because of it. |
| 1204 | `career/office.js:328` | Defense starts with your eyes. See your man and the ball at the same time. |
| 1205 | `career/office.js:329` | After a timeout, the first possession matters. Make it a clean one. |

| 1207 | `career/office.js:331` | Fundamentals don’t go out of style. Neither does footwork. |

| 1211 | `career/office.js:336` | Whoever doesn’t thank people hasn’t thanked Allah. Thank the uncles who stack the chairs. |

| 1218 | `career/office.js:343` | The best of you are those with the best character. Not the best jump shot. |
| 1219 | `career/office.js:344` | The strong believer is better and more beloved to Allah than the weak one, and there is good in both. Train your body and your heart. |

| 1223 | `career/office.js:348` | Don’t walk the earth with arrogance (17:37). Or the court. |
| 1224 | `career/office.js:349` | Those who show mercy are shown mercy by the Most Merciful. Help the other team up when they fall. |
| 1225 | `career/office.js:350` | Make things easy for people, not hard. That goes for teammates too. |
| 1226 | `career/office.js:351` | A good word is charity. Say one to someone who had a bad game. |

| 1230 | `career/office.js:355` | Gratitude in good times, patience in hard times. The believer wins either way. |
| 1231 | `career/office.js:356` | Knowledge is light. Keep visiting the bookshelf. |
| 1232 | `career/office.js:357` | The masjid isn’t the building. It’s the people who fill it five times a day. |
| 1233 | `career/office.js:358` | Honor your parents. Call them after the game, win or lose. |

| 1297 | `career/career-story.js:23` | **NEW** Think about what that means for an empty gym at 7am. |

| 1299 | `career/career-story.js:24` | **NEW** Especially then. Doing your best when nobody claps is the purest kind of effort. |

| 1302 | `career/career-story.js:25` | **NEW** It’s the opposite. You only have one audience to please, and He is the Most Merciful. |
| 1303 | `career/career-story.js:25` | **NEW** Do your best and leave the rest to Him. |
| 1304 | `career/career-story.js:26` | **NEW** Lesson three. The Prophet ﷺ said: “The strong one is not the one who overpowers others. The strong one is the one who controls himself when angry.” (Bukhari, Muslim) |
| 1305 | `career/career-story.js:27` | **NEW** What about when someone shoves me first? |
| 1306 | `career/career-story.js:27` | **NEW** Then you get to show real strength. Walk away, let the referee do his job. |
| 1307 | `career/career-story.js:27` | **NEW** If anger comes, the Prophet ﷺ taught us to seek refuge in Allah, and to sit down if you’re standing. |

| 1309 | `career/career-story.js:28` | **NEW** Play hard, yes. Fire in your legs is fine. Fire in your mouth and your hands is where it goes wrong. |
| 1310 | `career/career-story.js:28` | **NEW** Compete like a lion, carry yourself like a believer. |

| 1312 | `career/career-story.js:29` | **NEW** Gratitude is not just for wins. |
| 1313 | `career/career-story.js:30` | **NEW** How can I be grateful after a loss? |

| 1318 | `career/career-story.js:31` | **NEW** Stacking the chairs when the game is over. |
| 1319 | `career/career-story.js:32` | **NEW** Lesson five. “None of you truly believes until he loves for his brother what he loves for himself.” (Bukhari, Muslim) |

| 1656 | `career/career.js:311` | Assalamu alaikum! You must be the brother who just moved to Eastside. Welcome to Masjid Al-Amanah. |
| 1657 | `career/career.js:312` | Welcome to the gym! It's also the overflow musalla, the Sunday school, and the potluck hall. Watch out for folding chairs. |
| 1658 | `career/career.js:313` | The rim on the left is bent. Shoot at the right one. |
| 1659 | `career/career.js:314` | Beta, in 1987 I scored forty points in this gym. Nobody was keeping score, but I know. |
| 1660 | `career/career.js:315` | I'm Tariq, from the youth group. Somebody keeps score now. Every shot, every release, every assist. It's all on the sheet. |
| 1661 | `career/career.js:316` | And I'm Uncle Siddiq. Chai after Isha, and the chalkboard by the door keeps our streak. May it only go up. |
| 1662 | `career/career.js:317` | We're a small masjid, and we've never won the Metro Masjid League. But we play with adab, and we play for each other. |
| 1663 | `career/career.js:318` | You'll run the two-man game with me. Practice in the gym, earn Barakah Points, and get stronger. The league starts soon, in sha Allah. |
| 1664 | `career/career.js:319` | One shooting tip: let it go at the top of your jump. Watch the meter for the green. And don't force it with a hand in your face, pass instead. |
| 1665 | `career/career.js:322` | Al-Amanah is entering the league? Didn't your team lose to our youth group last year? |
| 1666 | `career/career.js:322` | That was a... strategic loss. |
| 1667 | `career/career.js:323` | Salaam, Jalal. May the better team win, and may we all pray Maghrib together after. |
| 1668 | `career/career.js:324` | Before every game I'll bring you a scouting report. Their record, their best player, one tip. Short. I promise. |
| 1669 | `career/career.js:325` | The State Masjid Championship. Eight of the best small masjids in the state. |
| 1670 | `career/career.js:325` | Travel light, pray on time, and don't eat gas station food before a game. |
| 1671 | `career/career.js:326` | Al-Burhan made it too. You beat us once. It will not happen twice. |
| 1672 | `career/career.js:327` | I packed two thermoses. One for us, one for Al-Burhan. Rivals still drink chai. |
| 1673 | `career/career.js:328` | Nationals. Brothers from masjids across the country, all in one gym. |
| 1674 | `career/career.js:328` | I looked up the other teams. They're good. |
| 1675 | `career/career.js:329` | We're good too! ...Right? |
| 1676 | `career/career.js:329` | We are. Our assist numbers went up every stage. I made a chart. |
| 1677 | `career/career.js:330` | We prepare, we make du'a, and we leave the rest to Allah. |
| 1678 | `career/career.js:331` | The World Ummah Cup. Istanbul, Kuala Lumpur, Lagos, Sarajevo. The whole ummah in one tournament. |
| 1679 | `career/career.js:332` | Whatever happens, you are representing all of us back home. Represent us well. |
| 1680 | `career/career.js:333` | Two teams from our country made it. Let us make sure one of us brings it home. |
| 1681 | `career/career.js:334` | Whatever the chalkboard says after this, I'm proud of all of you. Now go. The chai will wait. |
| 1682 | `career/career.js:337` | Good win. Now go help stack the chairs. Humility is also a muscle. |
| 1683 | `career/career.js:338` | Alhamdulillah. We thank Allah first, then we thank whoever grabbed those rebounds. |
| 1684 | `career/career.js:339` | Did you see that? DID YOU SEE THAT? |
| 1685 | `career/career.js:339` | Everyone saw it, Khalil. |
| 1686 | `career/career.js:340` | Very nice. In my day we also won. Stay hungry, but eat first. |
| 1687 | `career/career.js:343` | Sabr. A loss is a teacher if you let it be one. |
| 1688 | `career/career.js:343` | Free throws after Isha. I'll rebound for you. |
| 1689 | `career/career.js:344` | In 1987 we lost too. Then we had chai and came back. Have some chai. |
| 1690 | `career/career.js:344` | Next time we get them, bi idhnillah. |
| 1691 | `career/career.js:345` | Losing is not the shame. Losing your adab is the shame. You kept yours. |
| 1692 | `career/career.js:345` | We enter the tournament again. Same effort, more practice. |
| 1693 | `career/career.js:347` | Good game. You pushed us all the way. Do not quit now. |
| 1694 | `career/career.js:347` | He is right. Back to the gym. |
| 1695 | `career/career.js:349` | LOCAL CHAMPIONS! Put it on the whiteboard! Put it on every whiteboard! |
| 1696 | `career/career.js:349` | Alhamdulillah. First trophy in our history. Now, who is helping put the chairs back? |
| 1697 | `career/career.js:350` | Good game. You earned it. Go represent the city at State. |
| 1698 | `career/career.js:350` | The aunties are making biryani for the whole team. |
| 1699 | `career/career.js:351` | State champions, alhamdulillah. Remember how we felt after our first loss? Patience brought us here. |
| 1700 | `career/career.js:352` | We qualified for Nationals too. Save us a seat. |
| 1701 | `career/career.js:352` | Be humble in winning. The trophy is heavy, but a big ego is heavier. |
| 1702 | `career/career.js:353` | National champions. I don't know what to say. |
| 1703 | `career/career.js:353` | I do! ALHAMDULILLAH! |
| 1704 | `career/career.js:354` | We came from a gym with a bent rim. Now we meet masjids from all over the world. Let's be good guests and good ambassadors. |
| 1705 | `career/career.js:355` | World Ummah Cup champions. Subhanallah. |
| 1706 | `career/career.js:355` | Whatever we won, remember why we played: brotherhood, adab, and representing our community. |
| 1707 | `career/career.js:356` | In 1987 I scored forty points. But this... this is better. |
| 1708 | `career/career.js:356` | Can we fix the left rim now? |
| 1709 | `career/career.js:356` | We can fix the left rim now. |
| 1710 | `career/career.js:358` | Bend your knees on free throws. And your ego. |
| 1711 | `career/career.js:358` | Barakah is in sharing. Pass to the open brother. |
| 1712 | `career/career.js:358` | Box out. The rebound doesn't come to the one who waits. |
| 1713 | `career/career.js:359` | Hold shoot and let go at the top of your jump. Green on the meter is money. |
| 1714 | `career/career.js:359` | A contested shot is a gift to the other team. Find the open brother. |
| 1715 | `career/career.js:359` | Change direction while dribbling. The defender's ankles will need a break. |
| 1716 | `career/career.js:360` | Sprint at the rim and shoot: that is a dunk. Use it wisely. |
| 1717 | `career/career.js:360` | Pray before the game, not just after it. |
| 1718 | `career/career.js:360` | Defense is patience. Stay between your man and the rim. |
| 1719 | `career/career.js:361` | Help the man who falls, even if he wears the other jersey. |
| 1720 | `career/career.js:361` | Talk on defense. A quiet team gets screened. |
| 1721 | `career/career.js:361` | Stay low on defense. Tall legs, short temper, both are trouble. |
| 1722 | `career/career.js:362` | Shake hands before tip-off. Adab first, then basketball. |
| 1723 | `career/career.js:362` | **NEW** Drink water. Your legs will thank you in the last minute. |
| 1724 | `career/career.js:362` | A good pass makes two people happy. |
